# atpkg: specs recovered from its unit tests

> Mounted-volume acceptance is an explicit development task, outside ordinary tests:
> `targo --unverified bench -p atpkg --bench dmg_acceptance --profile test`.
> It may request macOS removable-volume consent. Every planned row must run; setup
> unavailability reports `COULD NOT RUN` (exit 3), an assertion failure reports `FAIL`
> (exit 1), and only completed controls pass. Release libraries omit these entrypoints.

> This file is atpkg's migration worklist. Each spec below is to be proved through Trust/Clean
> about the compiled Rust function, and then the tests it subsumes (§2) are deleted. No spec is
> proved yet, and the §4 actions are not applied.
>
> **No atpkg spec can be Certified yet.** On seal `150efe4a`, a strict `targo trust check -p atpkg`
> refuses the crate before any obligation runs: "verified Targo refuses unit atpkg … in-process
> proc-macro dependencies [aterm-serde-derive] … Evidence-grade verification currently enforces a
> no-proc-macro TCB boundary" (gap 9; atpkg derives through the `serde` = `aterm-serde` facade with
> its `derive` feature). Gap 9 therefore blocks every spec here, on top of the gaps each row lists.
>
> Pack-time relocation and the LINEAGE stamp/tree scan are atpkg-publish's, with their specs and
> tests (that crate's SPECS.md); atpkg has no `relocate` or `lineage` verb.
> `toolchain-discovery-order`, `toolchain-path-and-pin` and `toolchain-remedy-labels` describe
> `aterm_toolchain` code (atpkg re-exports it as `atpkg::toolchain`), which has no SPECS.md of its
> own; no test samples `toolchain-remedy-labels`.

Source: `crates/atpkg` (read only), plus the `aterm_toolchain` tests that sample its toolchain
specs. The specs come from reading each `#[test]` fn under `src/` and `tests/` (cfg-gated ones
included) and the code it reaches; nothing was compiled or run. Tests the map does not name (all
of `src/abrew/`, `src/converge/` and `src/alab_tests.rs` among them) sample no spec here yet.

Result: **495 specs**, sampled by **1501 tests** (§2). **6 tests are not spec samples** (§4).
The specs are listed in source-module order (activation and CLI, then flow/extract/gc/doctor, then
the store, network and signature layers, then the vendor-direct lane and the integration tests).

## 0. Notation used in the statements

Paths are repo-relative. A test is named `crates/atpkg/<file>::<fn>`.

**Store and prefix.**
- `l`: a `store::Layout` with prefix `p` (`l.prefix`).
- `build(n,b)` = `p/store/n/b`; `bin/` = `l.bin_dir()`; `agents/` = `l.agents_dir()`.
- `shim(t)` = `bin/<t><SHIM_SUFFIX>`; `alias(t)` = `alab-<t>`; the trust view is `p/rustup/trust`.
- `program_current(n)` = `store/n/current`, the *authority* symlink.
- `Complete(b)`: the sibling marker `<b>.ready` vouches for `b` (see `readiness-marker`).
- Scratch beside `b`: names exactly `<n>.incoming-<digits>` or `<n>.superseded-<digits>`.
- Aside of `b`: `<n>.noindex` beside it. On macOS a build that ships a Rust sysroot lives there,
  with `b` the relative link `<n>` -> `<n>.noindex` (`aterm_toolchain::layout`), out of Spotlight's index.
- Canonical build number: decimal `u64`, no sign, no leading zero (`store::parse_build_name`).
- `σ` / "the prefix state": the filesystem under `p`. That is store trees, markers, `current`
  links, `bin/` and `agents/` shims, `staging/<p>/` archives and `.part` files, leases, and the
  `<n>.vendor`, `<n>.shim-env`, `<n>.exposes` and declared-mode sidecars. The process table `R` comes with it
  (`gc::running_executables`, injected as a closure in tests).

**Shims.**
- `resolve_shim(f)`: the forwarding target parsed from `f`. It is `None` for a tombstone, a pending
  stub, or a dangling or foreign file.
- `shim_env_of(f)`: the exports parsed from `f`.
- `store_build_of(p, t)` = `Some((program, build))` iff `t` lies under `p/store/<program>/<canonical build>`
  or its aside,
  anchored to this prefix.
- `active_builds(l)`: program → build, read from the shims that resolve into the store.
- `ToolName::new(s)` is `Some` iff `shim_allowed(s)` (`tool-name-admission`).

**Reading and writing files.**
- `RegularFile(x)` / "bounded read": `x` is a regular non-symlink file within the stated byte
  cap, read with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK` (`metadata_io`). Any other file reads as absent and never blocks.
- "Atomic write": a temp sibling plus `rename(2)`, with no temp left on success or failure.
- "Writes nothing": no file under the named trees changes bytes, inode, nlink, ctime or mtime.

**Strings and quoting.**
- `q(x)`: POSIX single-quote escaping (`'` spelled `'\''`). Lemma: `/bin/sh` evaluating `q(x)` yields `x`.
- A *marker line* is `atpkg: ` ++ a `*_MARKER` constant ++ body. The exact wording of
  user-facing lines is a render table, not a spec. Statements keep only their structure
  (prefix, which items are named, which clauses appear).

**Index, signatures and passes.**
- *Channel view*: `manifest::Channel::for_target(TRIPLE)`, the per-target pin overlay.
  `gate::decide`, `current_build_ok` and the rollback gate all run over it.
- `min_build_for(p)` = `max(min_build, min_build_by_program[p])`.
- *A pass*: `cli::run_update_pass` / `run_recorded_update_pass`, run under injected seams (clock,
  anchor, trust, PATH, progress, flip policy). The test fetchers are `net::DirFetcher` (a signed
  `dir:` registry) and `vendor_direct::lane::world::Fake`.
- Crypto (ed25519, RSA PKCS#1 v1.5, SHA-1/256/512) is a specified external function. Signatures
  are taken as EUF-CMA (an axiom); digests follow FIPS 180-4, used as the definition.
- `⊕` / `⊖`: saturating `u64` add / sub.

**Vendor-direct lane.**
- `VENDORS`: `vendor_direct::table`'s compiled vendor table.
- `spec(p)`: the row for `p`.
- `bare(s)`: `vendor::bare_file_name`.
- `host(u)`: `vendor::https_host`.
- `allowed(p,u)`: `vendor_direct_url_allowed`.
- `V(f)`: the `Version` a field tuple builds.
  - Plain tier, `f=(a,b,c)`: id = `10^18 + a·10^12 + b·10^6 + c`.
  - Revision tier, `f=(a,b,c,n,s)`: id = `2·10^18 + a·10^15 + b·10^12 + c·10^9 + n·10^3 + s'`,
    where `s' = 0` for `None` and `k+1` for `Some(k)`.
- `VendorId` = `[10^18, 3·10^18)`; `is_vendor_build(b)` ⇔ `b ≥ 10^18`.
- `show(v)`: `Display`.
- `fields(v)`: the inverse of `V`.
- `yanked_P` / `legacy_P`: `Policy::is_yanked` / `is_legacy_yanked`.
- `cur` = `installed.version()`.
- `inst_yanked`: the installed build is yanked, by version or (legacy) by build number.

**Models.** Model names (`AtpkgPassStamps`, `AtpkgFullPassRule`, `AtpkgPublishedSpacing`,
`AtpkgPendingWait`, `AtpkgFlipQuiet`, `AtpkgIndexPublishWalk`, `AtpkgIndexProbeCooldown`,
`AtpkgIndexSuccessorSelection`, `AtpkgHeadWatchHosts`, `AtpkgIndexPendingPark`,
`AtpkgSessionIndex*`, `AtpkgIndexSharedHandoff`) are the models in
`crates/aterm-models-atpkg/src/models_atpkg_*.rs`. A *refinement* spec says the real code steps as
the model does at every reachable state, with the model's `Buggy` arm as the negative control.
`Denote(x)` in `tar-denotation` is a reference tar decoder, to be written as a Clean/Lean function
over 512-byte blocks.

**Gap numbers** are the rows of the AUDIT §7 table
(`docs/AUDIT-trust-for-test-migration-2026-10-15.md`):

| gap | covers |
|---|---|
| 1 | non-scalar carriers |
| 2 | `&self`/`&mut self` views |
| 3 | E6 body widening (calls, casts, bit ops, min/max, enum matches) |
| 4 | callee-ensures composition |
| 5 | quantified clauses |
| 7 | loops |
| 8 | effects (fs, process, FFI, unsafe) |
| 9 | the serde/proc-macro boundary |
| 10 | model→code refinement |
| 11 | generics |
| 12 | cross-crate citation |
| 13 | threads, closures, higher-order specs |
| 14 | Clean elaborator coverage |

Gap 0 (the installed seal) blocks every spec, so the column lists only the gaps beyond it, and
`0` means the seal is the only blocker.

**Process-edge specs** (`cli-*` and the integration-test halves of merged rows) are quantified over a
scratch `HOME`/prefix and the real `atpkg` binary. They are effect specs. Where a pure core
exists, the row names its pure-core sibling spec.

## 1. Specs (TSV)

Columns:
- `id`, `kind`, `functions`, `statement`;
- `blocking_gaps`: audit §7 numbers beyond gap 0;
- `primary_tests`: how many tests list this spec as primary in §2;
- `difficulty`: the Rust features the proof must handle.

Result: cdn Ok ⇒ Ok with cdn's bytes at dest and `<dest>.peer.part` removed; cdn Err with a good peer ⇒ the partial renamed to dest, Ok, and one `took … from LAN peer` line; cdn Err with none ⇒ cdn's Err, dest not made by a peer and no partial left.	1,3,7,8,13	5	scoped threads racing an injected FnOnce, two AtomicBool stops; TCP + fs effects; streaming SHA-256

So once cdn has failed, race_paced returns cdn's Err within a bound set by the pace and the peer count, however slowly a peer trickles, with neither dest nor `<dest>.peer.part` left. ### Pure decisions and their process edges

Some pairs are related but kept apart, because one is the pure decision and the other its process
edge. Proving the pure row is the core of the edge row in each case:

| pure decision | process edge |
|---|---|
| `reroute-plan-decision` | `cli-reroute` |
| `with-build-dir-rule` | `cli-build-cache` |
| `lane-answer` | `cli-lane-verb` |
| `selfupdate-plan` | `cli-selfupdate` |
| `argv-arity-gates` | `cli-usage` |
| `store-lock-waiting-protocol` | `cli-lock-wait` |
| `landing-sweep-effect` | `cli-landing` |
| `lease-holders-protocol` | `cli-lease` |
| `log-*` | `cli-pkglog` |

`shim-render-parse-roundtrip` (the activate-side reader agreement) and
`platform-sh-shim-roundtrip`/`platform-cmd-shim-roundtrip` (the renderers) also share a lemma
and should be proved together.

Gap 1 (non-scalar carriers) blocks 416 of the 495 specs, and gap 8 (effects) blocks 272. Those two
gaps decide the crate's migration. Of the 272 effect specs, the pure cores of most can be split out
and proved first (§6).

`primary` is the spec that retires the test. `also` lists the other specs the same test samples.

## 3. Coverage

- §2 maps 1501 tests to the 495 specs; 3 more are not spec samples (§4).
- The files with the most mapped tests are `cli_tests.rs` (188), `flow_tests.rs` (77), `seam.rs`
  (55), `doctor_tests.rs` (44) and `gc.rs` (40). Most of their tests sample effectful whole-pass
  specs (`default-set-decision-matrix`, `pass-verdict-and-record`, `doctor-verdict`,
  `flow-apply-decisions`, `seam-stale-link-heal`, `gc-run-never-deletes-protected`). Those are
  gap-8 work.
- The biggest collapses into one spec: `tar-denotation` (37, from `tarread.rs` plus the 21-test
  `tests/tar_corpus.rs`); `doctor-verdict` (20); `reroute-plan-decision` (17);
  `lane-refines-decide` (16); `flow-apply-decisions`, `gc-run-never-deletes-protected`,
  `sig-trust-chain` and `vnd-check-row` (15 each); `lane-no-bytes-before-vouch` (18);
  `seam-stale-link-heal` (14); `toolchain-discovery-order` (13);
  `default-set-decision-matrix`, `flow-archive-retention`, `dec-table` and `cli-lock-wait` (12 each).
- Several golden-corpus tests (`tests/tar_corpus.rs`, `serde_corpus_tests.rs`,
  `vendor_direct/stamp_corpus_tests.rs`) compare against answers recorded from a retired reference
  implementation. Their spec is a refinement to a reference *model*. The recording cannot be the
  proof oracle (§5.3).

## 4. Tests that are not spec samples

> Status 2026-10-28: converted `src/caller_shell.rs::probe_prints_the_invoking_shell_when_asked`, `src/seam.rs::probe_parks_in_the_view_until_killed`, and `tests/packages_log_lanes.rs::concurrent_appender_child` into non-`#[test]` stage entry helpers called from their owning tests.

Actions:
- delete: 0 tests remaining.
- move-to-bench: 3 tests: a cost budget and two host or OS probes.

| test | action | why |
|---|---|---|
| src/cli_tests.rs::an_idle_pass_writes_status_once_when_held | **move-to-bench** | Pins a write count (one status.toml write per held pass); the record's content is already covered by pass-verdict-and-record. A cost budget, not a spec. |
| src/landing.rs::the_verb_runs_the_bin_shim_as_a_child_with_its_real_code | **move-to-bench** | Windows-only integration test (cfg(all(test, windows))) that no machine of this repo runs; it checks cmd.exe's exit-code relay, a property of the OS, while the argv it would run is already landing-handover-parse. |
| src/platform/mod.rs::a_detached_program_starts_at_utility_and_a_waited_one_at_the_default_band | **move-to-bench** | Measures the OS scheduler band (`ps -o pri`) of a detached child; the code's part (the argv it builds) is platform-utility-clamp; the band itself is a kernel fact. |

## 5. Implied specs that look wrong, or disagree with the code

1. **Code and spec disagree.**
   - **Resolved — `gate::is_yanked` uses `store::parse_build_name`.** Yank entries now require canonical decimal build numbers (`0` or `[1-9][0-9]*`), rejecting `+4790` and `04790` to match the store's build grammar (`gate-is-yanked`).
   - **Resolved — `cost::disk_ok` exact `u128` sum.** `cost::disk_ok` now compares `(available as u128) >= (required as u128) + (free_floor as u128)` so `disk_ok(r, u64::MAX, f)` is `false` whenever `r + f > 2^64−1` (`cost-disk-ok`).
   - **Resolved — `toolchain::pinned_channel_in` tracks TOML table headers.** Only `channel = "…"` lines in `[toolchain]` (or top-level before any `[table]` header) are accepted; `channel` keys in other tables are ignored.
   - **Resolved — `BuildDate::parse` validates calendar month lengths and `second <= 59`.** Invalid calendar dates (`2026-02-29`, `1900-02-29`, `2026-04-31`, `2026-02-31`) and `:60Z` are now refused.
   - **Resolved — `resolve::staging_assets` and `gc::pursued_assets` agree with `verify_codex` on Windows.** `staging_assets` yields both `.zip` and `.tar.gz` local staging names for `codex` on Windows triples, and `gc::run_in` spares every candidate asset (and `.part`).
   - **Resolved — `doctor::withheld_summary(0, 0)` returns `""`.** Zero counts no longer render `"0 managed tool(s) cannot run"`.
   - **Resolved — `hooks::rc_hook_wired` doc accurately describes the `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]` helper.** Its doc comment no longer claims `doctor`/`which` call it.
   - **Resolved — `apply_defer_busy_flip_flag` and per-thread `main_entry` flags.** `DEFER_BUSY_FLIP`, `HEAD_WATCH_PASS`, and `PRIVATE_STATE_ROOT_LEAD` are now thread-local `Cell<bool>` slots reset on every `main_entry` call, and `the_defer_busy_flip_flag_is_stripped_and_picks_the_policy` checks both `WhenQuiet` and `Now` through `apply_defer_busy_flip_flag`.
2. **Stale names and labels.**
   - **Resolved:** Renamed `config::malformed_config_falls_back_to_defaults` to `config::malformed_config_reads_as_unreadable_fail_closed`.
   - **Resolved:** Stale "newest legacy pin" label on `vendor_direct/decide.rs::CEILING` removed.
3. **The oracle is a copy, a recording or test-only code.**
   - `net::the_rounds_land_what_the_sequential_walk_lands` compares against a hand-written
     `sequential_walk` that exists only in the test module. Per AGENTS.md it should be a `ty_model!`
     bound by a conformance test.
   - **Resolved:** `Anchor::as_str` was removed from `vendor_direct/table.rs`; `anchors_spell_as_their_record_strings` checks the serde wire strings directly against literal strings.
   - **Resolved:** `YankLatch::update_from_fresh_index` and `Policy::from_channel` are removed from `vendor_direct/policy.rs`; all policy and latch tests drive production `Policy::from_index`/`Policy::from_entries` and `YankLatch::update_from_index`, including a pre-generation (`roster_seq = None`) on-disk latch sample for `admits_at`'s `None` branch.
   - The `packages_log` status/pass-clock tests run against the `cfg(test)` `log_dir()`/`records()` seam,
     so `log-bound-store-and-pass-clock` must be proved about the production arm.
   - The golden corpora: `tests/tar_corpus.rs` folds answers into FNV digests recorded from the replaced
     `tar` crate, and `serde_corpus_tests.rs`/`stamp_corpus_tests.rs` replay the retired registry serde.
     A digest change cannot say which answer moved. The specs are `tar-denotation`,
     `status-index-manifest-serde` and `stp-serde`.
4. **Source-text scans.** None remain in atpkg's tests. A declarative verb table (`cli::TABLE`)
   drives dispatch, so the arity tests read data;
   `the_toolset_verb_changes_its_mind_before_its_pass` drives the verb over a scratch store;
   `ops::managed` is the one way to exec past a shim, so no door can take the path without its
   environment; `lock.rs` pins `CONTENDED_EXIT` alone. The tests that scanned for an order of
   calls keep their behavioural halves.
5. **Vacuous or host-dependent passes.** These are reported as passing when nothing was checked
   (AGENTS.md: report what actually ran):
   - `activate.rs::a_system_shaped_prefix_publishes_bin_traversable` and
     `cache.rs::a_system_shaped_prefix_leaves_the_cache_parent_traversable` take a
     system-shaped scratch prefix through a test-only seam (`store::system_shape`), so the
     system half of `prefix-shape-dir-mode` runs as any user.
   - When run as root: `ops.rs::a_shim_whose_target_cannot_be_stat_ed_is_still_live`.
   - Real mounted-image acceptance now lives in the explicit `dmg_acceptance` bench; missing `hdiutil`/`ditto` or image-creation failures report COULD NOT RUN. The ordinary DMG stage tests use injected folders and retain their picker, link, mode/ACL and release controls.
   - When `CARGO` is not Trust's targo: `tests/build_cache.rs::a_build_script_building_its_own_workspace_finishes_under_the_shim`.
   - Unreliable for other reasons:
     - `progress.rs::pid_alive_reads_an_unsignalable_foreign_pid_as_alive` never reaches its EPERM
       branch as root, and its reaped-child check can see a reused pid.
     - `caller_shell.rs::a_real_parent_shell_is_named_over_the_login_shell` needs two installed shells.
     - `lock.rs::the_holder_record_names_a_person_only_while_one_holds_the_store` depends on the
       parent pid's liveness.
6. **Timing used where an exact count belongs.** *(Resolved: `compat::tests::stamps` records `(dev, ino, mode)` with all nine 20 ms sleeps removed, and `cmd_selfupdate_refuses_forwards_and_never_takes_the_lock` uses a synthetic `#!/bin/sh` wrapper exiting `CONTENDED_EXIT` with no wall-clock bound or prebuilt binary.)*
7. **Prose pinning mixed into behaviour tests.** These tests pin exact sentences, glyphs or
   spellings; the specs keep only their structural content:
   - `cli.rs::an_unserved_architecture_and_a_removal_are_facts_an_exclusion_is_not`. aterm-gui quotes
     this sentence, so it should become a shared constant. It is duplicated in `a_marker_reads_as_a_sentence_on_a_terminal`.
   - The rollback, pin and marker byte-stable tests.
   - The `meter.rs` renders.
   - About a dozen `vendor_direct/lane.rs` verdict sentences.
   - The full-sentence asserts in `copies.rs`.
   - Half of `devtools.rs::the_words_pair_the_drag_with_plus_and_say_should_until_d14`, which
     duplicates aterm-gui's `devtools_helper::test_arms`.

   The `state.rs` row spellings are the exception: those strings are the cross-version
   `status.toml` wire format, so the format itself should be written down as the definition the
   `state-row-roundtrip` proof uses.
8. **Oversized or duplicated tests.**
   - `which_names_the_copy_that_runs_and_shadowing_is_read_per_path` is one ~520-line test sampling
     seven functions. Split it along `which-line-denotation` and `shadow-reconcile-says-never-records`.
   - **Resolved:** `seam::rustup_home_with` re-exports `aterm_toolchain::rustup::rustup_home_with`, so one implementation serves both.
   - **Resolved:** `tree.rs::tree_root_changes_on_any_mutation` (recompute-both-sides content/add/mode mutations) is narrowed and renamed to `tree.rs::tree_root_declared_uses_stored_mode_on_posix_and_declared_otherwise`, checking `tree_root_declared`'s `HAS_POSIX_MODES` vs `!HAS_POSIX_MODES` rule directly against the pinned cross-version `GOLDEN` vector.
   - `atpkg-publish`'s `artifact/relocate.rs::machine_local_classification_covers_both_oses` samples `lineage::stock_location`
     for most of its cases.

## 6. Proof order (easiest high-value first)

No spec is blocked by gap 0 alone: `doctor-listing-start` also needs gaps 1 and 9 (see its row and
the note at the top), and nothing in atpkg reaches a strict proof until gap 9 closes. The order
runs from specs whose bodies are scalar (gaps 2/3), then pure data (gaps 1/3/5), then pure code
with loops, views and callee composition. Effectful specs come last, after their pure core is
factored out, per `docs/PLAN-tests-to-specs-2026-10-15.md`'s "effects last".

### First proofs

1. **`doctor-listing-start`**, once gaps 1 and 9 close: three `bool`/`usize` branches, an
   `omega`-class proof. It retires one test, but it is the cheapest end-to-end check that the
   seal publishes a Lean-discharged row for atpkg at all.
2. **The scalar band:**
   - `cost-disk-ok`, `net-artifact-cap`, `attempted-pass-index-build`, `build-floor-admits`;
   - `pol-current`, `stp-recent`;
   - `config-enabled-resolver`, `config-install-consent`.

   These are `u64`/`i64`/`Option<bool>` straight-line or saturating arithmetic, blocked only by
   gaps 2/3. Each is near-free once E6 admits `saturating_*`/`min`/`max`. `build-floor-admits` is
   the rollback gate of index selection.
3. **The vendor `Version` family and its decision table:**
   - `ver-ctor`, `ver-order`, `ver-decode`: `const fn` `u64` arithmetic, blocked by gaps 3/5;
   - then `dec-table` with its corollaries `dec-safety` (3 tests), plus
     `gate-decide-truth-table`, `gate-current-build-ok` and `manifest-min-build-for` on the index side.

   These specs are the security core: no automatic downgrade without a signed yank, and a hold never
   keeps a yanked build. They retire about 30 tests, and every vendor-lane and update-pass
   refinement row builds on them.

Runner-up: the pure parsers and predicates of tier C/D:
- `vnd-host`, `vnd-names`, `vnd-check-row`;
- `extract-vet-entry` and `extract-vet-symlink-lexical`, the archive-slip guards;
- `index-probe-classify`, `lease-subject-stem`;
- `state-row-roundtrip`, `ver-text`, `macho-header-predicate`.

They need no effects, serde or models (gaps 1-5 and 7 at most), and they are the pure cores the
effectful `extract-slip-confinement`, `stage-fail-closed-invariant` and `lane-*` specs are built on.
